# Testing inventory

One row per pinned behavior, with the test that pins it and the evidence, grouped by area.
[testing.md](testing.md#what-is-verified) summarizes the rows for each area; the gaps are at the end.

## Core, the C ABI and the sanitizers

| Capability | Verified by |
|---|---|
| Core builds with zero Qt dependencies | CI `core-and-tests` job (Ubuntu, no Qt installed), plus the configure-time link check in the root `CMakeLists.txt`. That check was verified 2026-10-20 to fire on an injected `Qt6::Core` link, in core-only and in full configures. |
| Core and the Qt-free tests run clean under ASan, UBSan and LeakSanitizer | CI `core-sanitizers` (Linux, blocking). Its first run, 2026-10-20 (`8dcba3a`), found no memory errors or UB, but 5 tests closed their session without destroying it. With those fixed, run #85 (`6991bdd`) passed 74/74. |
| Every public C ABI header (`core.h`, `signals.h`, `signal_sets.h`, `profile.h`, `j2534.h`, `j2534_surrogate.h`, `serial.h`, `stream.h`, `vin.h`, `sim.h`) is valid strict C, each included on its own first | `c_abi` |
| Callback registration: dedup, idempotent unregister, reentrant register/unregister during dispatch; a dispatch reaches every callback once at any list length, and the list as it stood when the frame arrived (one removed mid-dispatch still called, one added not) | `CoreTest`, `.DispatchReachesEveryCallbackOnceAtAnyListLength`, `.DispatchReachesTheListAsItStoodWhenTheFrameArrived` |
| No transport's worker allocates per frame, so no `bad_alloc` can leave a worker: a dispatch to up to four callbacks takes its snapshot on the stack, and past start-up the mock's, replay's (looping, the file reopened each pass, under `obd2`) and the J2534 worker's (through the surrogate, against the fake: the probe, identification and the rotation) frames cost no `operator new`, with a values callback registered, so the decode and its batch are counted too. `tests/new_counter.h` counts `operator new` per thread, read from a callback on the worker; a fifth callback is seen allocating, so the count isn't vacuous | `CoreTest.DispatchToAShortListAllocatesNothing`, `.WorkersAllocateNothingPerFrame`, `J2534AdoptionTest.TheWorkerAllocatesNothingPerFrame`. Each fails against the per-frame vector copy; the dispatch cases against a heap path that stops at four, four sent to the heap, and a dispatch over the live list; the worker case against a replay worker that reallocates its line buffer per row. Counts `operator new` only: `malloc` and `fopen` report failure as null, which the code checks |
| Callbacks run on the worker thread | `CoreTest`, through `ecuio_session_start` |
| Decoded values (ADR-0024): a frame's batch holds every def it decodes, in the set's order, equal to direct `ecuio_decode_*` calls, with the frame's time and ID; a def that can't decode is left out, and a frame that decodes nothing or a raw session delivers no batch. The values callback registers as the frame callback does, runs after the frame callbacks for each frame, and sees its list as it stood when the frame arrived, on and off the stack. Each text value has a slot of its own, counted at create from the set (unkeyed text defs plus, per key shape, the largest group sharing a key), and the slots start again each frame. A `uds-generic` scanned DID decodes from the set built at create. Every batch over the vehicle fixture under `obd2` and the mock's cycle equals the direct decode, followed by the computed values its frame fed (none under `obd2`, the mock's boost after every frame) | `CoreTest.ValuesCallbackDeliversEachFramesDecodeWithItsTime`, `.ValuesCallbackRegistersAsTheFrameCallbackDoes`, `.ValuesFollowTheFrameCallbacks`, `.ValuesReachTheListAsItStoodWhenTheFrameArrived`, `.ValuesGiveEachTextValueASlotOfItsOwn`, `.ValuesOfAScannedDidComeFromTheSetBuiltAtCreate`, `.ValuesMatchDirectDecodesOfEveryFrame`. Each fails against a mutant: one slot for every text value, a slot count without the unkeyed defs, shape groups taken as the largest instead of added, slots that never restart, the frame callbacks skipped, the live values list, a batch without the frame's time, a failed decode delivered as 0 |
| The defs a frame is checked against (`core/src/def_index.h`): for any frame, exactly the defs `ecuio_def_applies_to_frame` takes over the whole set, in the set's order, over sets mixing unkeyed defs and every key shape on shared IDs, keys shared between defs, against frames too short for a key, error reports and IDs no def listens on | `DefIndexTest.FindsExactlyTheDefsThatApplyInTheSetsOrder` (mutation-checked 2026-10-31: the runs never sorted, unkeyed defs left out, a key ending at the frame's end refused, error reports found); the batches through it: `CoreTest.Values*`, `.APacked*` |
| The bridge stages values from the worker (ADR-0024): a frame dropped at the in-flight cap keeps its values, so with the event loop held while 5,000 frames flood in, thousands drop, yet the recording holds every frame and every row's value in order, its last note says how many frames the Activity page skipped, and the row shows the last; past a window's sample limit the row still shows the newest, the Delivery line counts the values past the limit, and the recording holds every one | `BridgeTest.ValuesSurviveFramesDroppedAtTheCap`, `.ValuesPastTheWindowsLimitAreCountedAndLogged`. Each fails against a mutant: values never staged, the newest lost past the limit, the limit never counted, a staging never reset, no skipped-frames note (2026-11-03). The first also fails when the dropped-frame count is zeroed after the worker starts |
| The earlier name's preferences carried over: every key copied once into an empty store, never over a store that holds keys, the earlier store left as it was, nothing done while the names are equal (ADR-0001's appendix of 2026-10-28) | `BridgeTest.CarryOverCopiesTheEarlierPreferencesOnce`, `.CarryOverNeverWritesOverAStoreThatHasPreferences`, both against a scratch ini store; mutation-checked. The CLI's `slept` line follows the same rule; a SocketCAN frame after a sleep keeps the kernel's gap | `BridgeTest.ASleepWhileConnectedIsLoggedWithItsLength` (through `addSleptForTesting`), `CliSleepTest.ARiseOfASecondInOnePollIsASleep`, `SocketCanTest.ASleepKeepsItsGap`. | Log order toggle (newest first / oldest first). | The frame log's ring at its cap: the newest 10k rows in arrival order either way, each with its own frame's bytes and length, the oldest leaving from its end and the new frame arriving at the other, still in order after going round oldest-first; the frame counters reach QML on the 100 ms tick, not per frame, and end exact at disconnect | `BridgeTest.FullFrameLogKeepsTheNewestInOrder`, `.FrameCountsAreReportedOnTheTick`. Each fails against a slot that ignores the oldest, a removal at the newest end, and counters emitted per frame. | Frames after a loss at the adapter (`ECUIO_FRAME_FLAG_OVERFLOW`): counted in `onFrame`, reported on the tick as `overflowFrames`, the first of a session with one activity-log line; the count readable after the disconnect and started again by the next connect | `BridgeTest.FramesAfterALossAtTheAdapterAreCountedAndLoggedOnce`, checked against a mutation of the once-a-session line and of the reset at connect (2026-10-27). The Activity page's `overflowLabel` shows it in the frame view |
| The frame log's rows hold a payload up to 64 bytes inline and a longer one in a heap block only while the row is shown: a ring of 200-byte frames rounded by 27-byte ones holds no heap bytes, 64 bytes stays inline and 65 spills; Clear keeps the slots and frees the blocks, and a ring cleared before it filled grows only as far as it's used | `BridgeTest.FrameLogHoldsLongPayloadsOnlyWhileShown` (the model directly, through its slot and heap-byte seams). Each of five mutations fails it: a short frame keeping the block, Clear dropping the ring or keeping the blocks, growth by the cap alone, and the boundary at 63. Activity page smoke, 2026-10-25: under `mock://demo` before and after Clear, and the fake's 203-byte `62 F1 A0` replies between 5-byte `62 1E 1C` ones, each row with its own length and bytes |
| The activity log's "Delivery:" line at disconnect: frames received, dropped at the in-flight cap and still queued, then how long frames took to reach the GUI thread and values the model (median, p99, max), the line naming exactly the figures the bridge kept. Over the engine-start fixture at full speed every frame is accounted for and every handled frame and staged value timed. A quantile reads at the top of its bucket: exact below 16 us, at most 12.5 % high past that, the maximum exact | `BridgeTest.LatencyStatsReadsQuantilesWithinABucket`, `.DeliveryTextNamesFramesDropsAndTimes`, `.DisconnectLogsTheDelivery`; the line's place by `.DisconnectTearsDownOffGuiThread` and `.RecordingWritesFramesValuesAndEvents` (`events.log`). The fixture case failed with either timing left out (2026-10-26). App smoke under `mock://demo`, 2026-10-26: "Delivery: 98 frames, none dropped; to the GUI thread in median 95 us, p99 8.7 ms, max 8.7 ms; 819 values, to the model in median 53 ms, p99 109 ms, max 109 ms" |
| The C ABI against the newest release (`scripts/abi-check.sh`, libabigail): a removed or changed function, a field moved, resized or retyped, a struct's size changed and an enum value renumbered fail; an addition, an appended enum value and growth into a struct's reserved room pass. The headers' half (`scripts/api_check.py`): a member renamed or retyped at the same size, a `#define` or enumerator renumbered or gone, and a parameter or return type changed fail; a parameter renamed and comments or formatting pass | CI's C ABI step in `core-and-tests`, against v0.3.3 on the hosted runner (2026-10-29): unchanged; against v0.3.2 in WSL (2026-10-29): 10 functions and a variable added, passes. Mutants of the headers (2026-10-29): two frame fields swapped, an enum value renumbered, a function removed, a struct grown past its size, the reserved room used beside a swap of two other fields, and a field inserted that grew the struct all fail; a field in a reserved array, a reserved scalar taken, an enum value appended and no change pass. A one-element reserved array taken whole as its element (the diagnostics report's `cycle`) passes, against v0.3.5 in WSL (2026-11-01); taken as another type, or with a member outside the room renamed, it fails. `AbiReportTest.SelfTest` pins the judge's rules over those reports (the report CI read, 2026-11-01, among them; each of the two new rules failed the self-test as a mutant). The headers' half (2026-11-04): every release from v0.3.4 to v0.4.0 passes against the one before, and HEAD against v0.4.0; mutants of the real headers (`ecuio_frame.flags` signed, a session info member renamed, `ECUIO_DECODE_ERR_PAST_PAYLOAD` renumbered, a parameter retyped) fail, a parameter renamed passes; the renumbered `#define`, which libabigail calls unchanged, fails the whole script in WSL (exit 1). `ApiCheckTest.SelfTest` pins its rules (24 cases) |

## Mock and replay

| Capability | Verified by |
|---|---|
| What the demo sends and what it says it never sends: under a profile that reads Service 01 PIDs the mock's ECU is the simulated ECU over its drive cycle, so the demo car's PIDs have values (a case for each group of the PIDs it answers, and the counts since a clear following a clear) and the set's other Service 01 signals, and what is computed from them, are hidden as not sent ([ADR-0039](adr/0039-full-service01-coverage.md)'s appendix C); a computed signal is listed unsupported or absent when the inputs it doesn't get leave its program no value; nothing is disclaimed under a profile with no PIDs; a complete walk whose chain ends early lists every later PID unsupported, and an unfinished walk judges no range it didn't answer (section 6) | `CoreTest.TheMockSendsTheDemoCarsPidsAndDisclaimsTheRest`, `.TheDemoCarUnderObd2SendsItsValues`, `.TheDemoCarsCountsSinceAClearFollowTheClear`, `.TheMockDisclaimsNothingUnderAProfileWithNoPids`, `.AComputedSignalWithAnAbsentInputIsAbsentToo`, `MockPidsTest`, `PidSupportTest.AComputedSignalFollowsItsInputs`, `.AComputedSignalListedOnlyByAnInputItCantDoWithout`, `ComputedTest.AProgramLacksAValueWithTheInputsItCantDoWithout`, `CoreTest.AComputedSignalReadingAnInputOnlyInABranchIsNotListedWithIt`, `.AChainEndedEarlyMarksEveryLaterPidUnsupported`, `.AnUnfinishedWalkJudgesNoRangeItDidntAnswer`, `BridgeTest.TheMockSendsTheDemoCarsPidsAndHidesTheRest`, `QmlTest.dashboard`'s `test_theDemoCarsGaugesGetValuesUnderObd2` |
| The mock's other ECUs: a signal file reading Service 01 on the engine ECU and on a second ECU gets two channels, the second walked to bitmaps that list no data PID, so its signal is listed unsupported and only the engine ECU's has values; a profile with no other ECU keeps one channel, and the channels stop at `ECUIO_MAX_CHANNELS` | `CoreTest.TheMockDeclaresTheSetsOtherEcusWhichSupportNoPid`, `CliGoldenTest.StreamReadsThroughAPluginsVariant` (the example's second ECU in its session line) |
| A replay reads with its recording's own signal file ([ADR-0051](adr/0051-signal-files.md)'s appendix of 2026-11-06): the app's Replay of a recording uses the store's file when it holds the same bytes, and the recording's copy as `rec.<16 hex>` once the vehicle's file has changed or been removed, still decoding the recorded signal; a recording of that replay holds the copy; a copy missing or no longer matching its hash leaves the URI as given, with a log line; `havne-cli stream replay://` with no `--signals` selects the recorded profile and gives the recording run's values lines, under its own name too, leaves the copy unused under another profile, and replays raw once the copy changed, saying why; another `.csv` in the folder is a frames file of its own and replays raw, and a query core refuses (`?` alone, `&&`) is refused with the copy there as without it; the header's lines, the folder (`frames.csv` itself, never another `.csv`) and the profile rewrite (an empty pair kept) | `BridgeTest.ReplayDecodesWithTheRecordingsOwnSignalFile`, `.ReplayOfAChangedSignalFileCopyDecodesAsLoaded`, `CliReplayTest.AReplayReadsWithItsOwnSignalFile` (`tests/cli_replay_signals.py`), `CliRecordingSignalsTest.*` (`test_cli`). Mutation-checked (2026-11-06): the store's profile taken for any bytes, a replay's recording without the copy, the hash unchecked (the app's and the CLI's), the CLI's copy never used, its own name ignored; another `.csv` taking the folder's copy, an empty pair dropped |
| A replay through the CLI is complete at `speed=max` however slow its reader: with the ring shrunk to a few lines and output read 4 KB at a time, the run ends with every frame and no drop | `CliGoldenTest.ReplayRunsToItsLastFrame` (`HAVNE_CLI_RING_BYTES`, `golden.py --slow`). Fails against a replay that drops instead of waiting (6,567 frames dropped), but only with the slow reader: Python reads fast enough that the small ring alone never fills |
| Mock frames decode to physically plausible values; the mock's boost (computed, ADR-0051 section 5) arrives in every batch as its MAP minus its baro, a vacuum or zero | `CoreTest.MockFramesDecodeAgainstMockSignalSet`, `.MockBoostIsMapMinusBaro`, `.MockDriveCycleSignalsStayPhysical`, `.MockDriveCycleEveryTickRoundTripsThroughTheMockSet`. Mutation-checked 2026-11-05 (8: boost a sum, its inputs swapped, MAP never worked out, a stopped engine pumping a vacuum, the intake gaining pressure with flow, MAP and baro swapped on the wire, no residual pressure, the padding not zero) |
| The mock worker keeps real time: over 4 s its frames' timestamps average one 50 ms tick apart, within the bound its deadline schedule guarantees (one interval per run between stalls and restarts, spread over the gaps), so the drive cycle plays at the speed its physics assume. A host that stalls so often the bound can't see a worker 10 % slow skips (the macos-build runner) | `CoreTest.MockFramesKeepTheTickInterval`. A fixed 50 ms sleep after each tick (62.6 ms on Windows) and a schedule 10 % slow (55.0 ms) each failed it against a bound of 51.6 (2026-10-26). On Linux a fixed sleep overshoots by a fraction of a millisecond, which the test can't tell from a deadline |
| Mock drive cycle, stepped deterministically: every phase in order; every loop starts with 15 s of key on and the engine stopped (0 rpm once it has run down, no air, no speed, lambda lean, the warm coolant not falling with the pump stopped), then cranks near 220 rpm and runs at its idle within 2 s; the 2nd- and 3rd-gear pulls hold the fuel-cut limiter exactly 5 s, rpm past it on first contact and then sawing under it while road speed holds, the blade open, load at 94 % or more, MAF on its WOT plateau and lambda lean, the cycle mostly in cut; the 3rd-gear pull is longer and faster and heats the coolant and oil, and the boost's charge heats the intake air more than 5 deg C over ambient, never more than the compressor's rise past the intercooler plus 5; a 100 km/h cruise in 4th at 2850-3100 rpm in closed loop; the cold start's fast idle near 1430 rpm on extra air, then 850-900 rpm; the launch slips the clutch above 3000 rpm with the tires at their peak slip, then traction control holds them to the grip, so they hook up and never spin again; the drag run crosses 402 m in 11.5-13.5 s at 190 km/h or more, under 4th gear's limiter speed; each WOT pull (2nd, 3rd, the quarter mile) passes 12 psi of boost above the spool rpm, with none in a road pull's first two ticks, and every lift from WOT vents it through the blow-off valve, MAP below the outside air within three ticks; only the first loop runs cold (the thermostat stays shut, the oil trails the coolant), the coolant never falls below 85 deg C from the third, and in the sixth it holds 85-97 deg C at cruise, peaks at 100-105 deg C during the pulls and the quarter mile with the fan cutting in, the oil holds 100-125 deg C and ends the loop above the coolant, and a quarter mile moves it 2-9 deg C; at every tick of three loops, rpm is locked to wheel speed in gear (within the crank reading's jitter), never below 1000 rpm in gear, no more than 50 km/h with the clutch in, MAF matches rpm and load below full load and at full load lies between WOT's airflow and what the boost's charge adds to it, the manifold's pressure never above the outside air plus the wastegate's most boost and above the outside air only at full load above the spool rpm, the outside air itself with the engine stopped, with no boost the residual plus load's share of the outside air less the intake's loss once the engine pumps a full vacuum, within 10 % of the outside air at 95 % load and 25-50 kPa at idle, lambda is lean exactly when fuel is cut, the rev limit cuts fuel the tick after rpm meets it in any phase, and no temperature moves more than 0.5 deg C a tick; the encoded rpm never reads the same for more than half a second (a stopped engine's 0 aside), and away from shifts, the limiter and the launch it never steps more than 150 rpm a tick, phase changes, the stop and the start included; every tick round-trips through the mock set without clamping, and boost from the wire's MAP and baro stays between the envelope's vacuum and the wastegate's most | `CoreTest.MockDriveCycle*`. Each new check failed against its mutation (checked by hand, 2026-10-24): the limiter's load dipping with the saw, the clutch going in at 3000 rpm, lambda ignoring fuel cut, a lift off the limiter left fueled at the limit, the idle without its hunting, the crank reading without its jitter, the engine falling to idle unlimited when the clutch goes in. The warm-loop checks failed against a stronger radiator, no piston heat into the oil, a lighter sump, less heat into the coolant, a fan out of reach, a thermostat opening at 70 deg C, and the oil's friction heat counted on top of the fuel's (2026-10-25). The key-on checks failed against the engine left running, the phase skipped, the catch's rise uncapped and the water pump left running with the engine stopped (2026-10-30) |

Over a real socket, the fake ELM327 behind a loopback listener: a whole session through the OS's opener, the wire log saying "over TCP"; a read with nothing to read returns after its timeout; the far side closing is a failed port, one hard failure, and the socket connects again on the backoff with the values back and no reconnect; nothing listening is `LOAD_FAILED` "the connection was refused" within the 2 s limit (on Windows, whose SYN retries after a refusal take 2 s by default, about 1 s). | A SocketCAN session hears the bus as it is: frames in order with their IDs, flags (29-bit, remote, CAN FD with its bit-rate switch) and bytes; `?filter=`'s IDs only, 11-bit and 29-bit kept apart; the mock's drive cycle played onto `vcan0` decoding through `?profile=mock` to the mock transport's own values; a quiet bus `VEHICLE_TIMEOUT` with no episode, then `OK`; the first frame queued after a full socket's loss flagged `OVERFLOW`; no allocation on the worker per frame | `SocketCanBusTest.*` on `vcan0` (WSL's kernel 6.18 and CI's `linux-build`). Mutation-checked 2026-10-29: no `OVERFLOW` flag, a filter mask without the width bit and no quiet windows each fail their case |
| SocketCAN's timestamps keep the kernel's spacing: a batch read at once, or read late after a stall, keeps its receive times; a frame stamped up to 100 ms before the newest, read after it, takes the newest's time and keeps the stall out; a wall-clock step either way gives way to the host's delta; never decreasing | `SocketCanTest.TheClock*`, `.AWallClockStep*` (fails against host time alone, 2026-10-29), `.AFrameReadOutOfOrderTakesTheNewestTime` (fails with the reordering taken for a step, 2026-11-03) |
| ISO-TP (ADR-0040 section 1), receiving: a Single Frame is its message, unpadded when it holds its length; a First Frame and its Consecutive Frames are one message, across the sequence's wrap and to 4095 bytes; eight IDs interleave; a gap, a short frame, a new First or Single Frame on the same ID, eviction and a Consecutive Frame later than N_Cr (150 ms) each drop the message, counted; an untimed receiver waits for the reply's end; the Flow Control frame is ClearToSend, block size 0, STmin 0, 8 bytes. Sending: a Single Frame padded to 8 bytes; a First Frame then Flow Control within N_Bs (75 ms); STmin kept (the microsecond values, a reserved value as 127 ms); a block size asks for Flow Control again; wait frames restart N_Bs up to 10, a ClearToSend resetting the count; an overflow or a bad flow status ends the send | `IsoTpTest` (mutation-checked 2026-10-31: no sequence check, no N_Cr, the block size ignored, a reserved STmin as 0, waits unbounded, the wait count kept); a functional request's Flow Control on the physical request ID (`isotp_flow_control_id`; mutation-checked 2026-11-01: the request's own ID fails it, the J2534 case and `SimBusTest.AFunctionalRequestsSegmentedReplyCompletes`) |
| The interface going down is one hard-failure episode, and the session recovers when it's back up, with no reconnect | `SocketCanBusTest.TheInterfaceGoingDownIsAHardFailureThatRecovers`, as root: CI's `core-sanitizers` runs it under `sudo`; it skips elsewhere |
| A frame queued after a full socket's loss carries the overflow flag, and nothing before it does: the worker is held on a gate opened after the flood, so the first read can't free a slot mid-flood; no link failure | `SocketCanBusTest.FramesLostToAFullSocketAreFlagged` |
| The modes a CAN controller's driver offers (ADR-0040's appendix "the desk bus", amended 2026-11-03): read from `IFLA_CAN_CTRLMODE_EXT` when the kernel reports it (from 6.0) and ruling nothing out when it doesn't; a requesting session through a driver without one-shot mode, or a listening one without listen-only mode, refused before any other check, its words saying so and never naming the missing mode's command; the command's "(where its driver offers it)" only when the kernel doesn't say; `listen-only off` in the listen-only controller's command; discovery's state (from 1, unknown past the list or with none), modes and the driver's modes, a vcan's none; ip link's words for each state and mode; `havne-cli devices`' `can` line (null where nothing is known, an unnamed mode bit as hex); Settings' rows for each case | `SocketCanTest.TheModesADriverOffersAreRead`, `.ADriverWithoutTheModeANeedsIsRefusedFirst`, `.ARefusalNamesOnlyTheModesTheDriverOffers`, `.StatesAndModesHaveIpLinksNames`, `.DiscoveryListsControllersAndVcanOnly`, `c_abi`, `CliCanTest.ALineSaysWhatAConnectIsJudgedOn`, `QmlTest.settings_uri` (`test_canRowsFillASocketcanUri`). Mutation-checked (2026-11-03, nine): nothing lacking, the nest unread, either mode judged after the link's state, the caveat always, no modes-known flag, the state from 0, the CLI's driver modes always null, Settings ignoring what the driver offers. A real controller's, read through discovery alone (nothing opened or sent): the desk's USB2CAN (`gs_usb`) under WSL's 6.18 kernel lists loopback, listen-only and cc-len8-dlc, no one-shot, and its state (`havne-cli devices`, 2026-11-03) |
| A SocketCAN session that requests sends only through a controller that can stop at once (ADR-0040 section 2): normal mode, the rate `?baud=` states (none stated is refused), `restart-ms` 0, one-shot mode while that's the rule, and error-active; a listen-only one, or one reporting no mode, state or restart time, is refused; `vcan` always passes, its rate unchecked. The first request proves the rate (section 3): its echo, then its reply or 25 ms (N_As) with no error frame, proves it; an error before then, after the echo too, or three requests unseen ends the sending, and once proven nothing more changes there; the session's kernel filter holds each channel's reply and request IDs once, of their width | `SocketCanTest.OnlyAControllerThatCanStopAtOnceMaySend`, `.AnEchoThenAReplyOrQuietProvesTheRateAndAnErrorHalts`, `.ARequestingSessionFiltersItsChannelsIds` over canned netlink messages; on `vcan0` started unproven through a test-only switch, `SocketCanRequestTest.AnUnprovenSessionIsProvenByTheEcusReply`, `.AnUnprovenSessionIsProvenByQuietAfterItsEcho` and `.AnErrorFrameAfterTheFirstEchoHalts` (mutation-checked 2026-11-02: no reply settle, no quiet settle, the echo proving at once). ## J2534 and the surrogate

| Capability | Verified by |
|---|---|
| A scheme switch keeps `keepalive_ms` and `gap` for J2534, ELM327 and SocketCAN | `QmlTest.settings_uri`' `test_anotherSchemeKeepsKeepaliveMsAndGap` |
| The surrogate holds a 1 ms timer: through the fake as the VX (a reply 4 ms after its write, which the fake waits for in the timer's steps; the read ends at it), every request's median latency is under 14 ms, where Windows' default 15.6 ms step put it at 15.4 to 16.4 ([ADR-0042](adr/0042-reply-timing.md)'s appendix of 2026-11-01) | `J2534HealthTest.VxRepliesAreSeenWhenTheSliceEndsNotTheTimersStep` (mutation-checked 2026-11-01: no `timeBeginPeriod` failed every request) |
| A J2534 read ends at its reply and never asks the vendor to wait 0 ms: through the fake as the VX, a reply 15 ms after its write (past the 10 ms slice) reads under 25 ms (16.4 measured), where a 0 ms re-read, which the VX holds 50 ms, put it at 63; a 4 ms reply reads under 10 ms (5.1 measured), where a read held to its slice put it at 11.3, and a call for the rest of a batch, which the fake as the VX holds 10 ms as the car did, past the bound; queued messages come back one a read with no hold; the read after an indication past the deadline makes one more call, which waits 1 ms, not 0, and takes the reply behind it (judged from the fake's own record of its calls and what each asked, not from a wall clock that a loaded machine stretches); a loss seen after a read's last frame flags the next read's frame, and a read that fails after it reports the failure at once and keeps the loss for the next frame; a reconnected channel starts with no loss; a vendor that returns more messages than a call asked has the rest counted lost | `J2534HealthTest.VxAReplyPastTheSliceIsNotHeldByAZeroTimeoutRead`, `.VxAReplyEndsTheReadThatWaitsForIt`, `.VxQueuedMessagesComeBackWithoutAHold`, `.VxTheReadAfterAnIndicationPastTheDeadlineStillWaits`, `.VxTheReadAfterAnIndicationPastTheDeadlineTakesTheReplyBehindIt`, `.VxALossAfterAReadsLastFrameFlagsTheNextRead`, `.ALossCarriedIntoAFailedReadLetsTheFailureThrough`, `.AReconnectedChannelStartsWithNoLoss`, `.AVendorThatReturnsMoreThanAskedFlagsTheLoss` (mutation-checked 2026-11-04: a 0 ms re-read, a first call for 16, a 0 ms drain, no drain and the loss not carried each failed one; 2026-11-06, against the fake's record: the call after the deadline asking 0 ms and an extra call after each failed the deadline cases) |

## Decoding, signal files and plugins

| Capability | Verified by |
|---|---|
| The strict JSON reader that signal files go through ([ADR-0051](adr/0051-signal-files.md) section 1, `core/src/json.h`): every type, each value and key at its line and column (columns in characters; a `\r\n` or a lone `\r` one line break), objects in file order, escapes and surrogate pairs decoded, an integer exact within `int64_t` and a number read without the locale (a comma locale where the machine has one); refused with the line, column and words: a duplicate key (also in an object of a thousand members, and spelled with an escape), comments, trailing commas, NaN and Infinity, a leading zero or `+`, a number past the double range, single quotes, a raw control character in a string, invalid UTF-8 (overlong forms, encoded surrogates, past U+10FFFF, cut sequences, stray continuation bytes), a lone surrogate in a `\u` escape, an unknown escape, a byte order mark, text after the value, an input ending inside a string, array or object (naming where it opened), nesting past 32 and input past 1 MiB (both at their edges, and a bound the caller sets); the densest input within the bound parses. Core's writer (`ecuio_json::write`): two spaces a level, every escape, and what it writes reads back as the same document, each number of its kind and sign (`-0`, `-0.0`, `1.0`, a whole number past `int64_t`, a 16-digit one written with a fraction) in the shortest text that reads it back. A number whose exponent lies far past the double range costs what any number does (`parse_number` takes it as infinite or zero at once: a megabyte of `1e-999999` reads within ten times the time of ordinary numbers), and a `\u` escape's low surrogate needs its own backslash. A number reads as the double nearest it: digit by digit where that is exact (every digit kept, under 2^53, a power of ten within 22), else through `strtod` in the C locale (`_strtod_l`, `strtod_l`) on the same value written plainly; a 17-digit shortest text (`1.0212934580499022e-07`), 100,000 random doubles through `%.17g`, a fraction of 999,999 zeros before an exponent of a million, and a comma locale, and a plugin with such a scale loads and reads back as its set. The words that quote a file's text (`quoted`) show `?` for a character that hides what a line says: a C1 control, U+2028 and U+2029, the direction marks (U+061C among them), embeddings and overrides, the isolates, and the characters with no width (U+00AD, U+200B-U+200D, U+2060-U+2064, U+FEFF), their neighbors kept; an OBDb name makes each a space | `JsonTest.*`, `RecordingRowsTest.AnExponentFarPastTheRangeIsZeroOrInfinite`, `.ANumberIsTheDoubleNearestIt` (`test_core`), `PluginTest.ASeventeenDigitScaleReadsBackAsItsSet` (`test_signals`), `fuzz_json` (seeded by `fuzz/seeds.py`; 2026-11-05: Clang with ASan and UBSan in WSL, about 9.3 million inputs in 180 s, nothing found; with the writer's check, 4.8 million in 241 s). Mutation-checked (2026-11-05, 25): duplicate keys unchecked (both paths, the large object's set, the set missing the first keys), comments, trailing commas in an array and an object, `-Infinity`, leading zeros, overlong C0/C1 and 3-byte forms, encoded surrogates, past U+10FFFF, a bad continuation byte, a lone low surrogate, a high one with no low, the byte order mark, the depth and size bounds one past, raw control characters, `int64_t`'s upper edge, the double range, text after the value, a surrogate pair combined wrong, an escape left undecoded; the writer (2026-11-05, 8): a quote unescaped, a control character's escape cut, `-0`'s sign lost, no fraction kept, `.0` past 15 digits, a whole number past `int64_t` in its shortest text, one level less indent, 15 digits only; the far exponent, the escape and `quoted` (2026-11-05, 6): either far exponent unclamped, the low surrogate's backslash unchecked, `quoted` showing hidden characters as they are, the isolates, U+200F; the nearest double (2026-11-06, 4): the digit-by-digit path past 2^53, the far exponent's shortcut gone, a fraction's leading zeros uncounted, the process's locale in place of the C one; 2026-11-06, 4: U+061C, U+FEFF, the zero widths, the soft hyphen |

| A signal's gauge ([ADR-0051](adr/0051-signal-files.md)'s appendix "a signal's gauge"): `min`, `max` and both warning levels kept as the file wrote them, a level left out 0 with its flag clear; a level at either end of the range, a range wider than the field, overlapping it or meeting one end of it, `notes` and `x-` keys inside it all taken; every refusal its words with the line and column (not an object, `min` or `max` missing, a value of the wrong type, an unknown key, `min` not below `max`, a level outside the range, levels equal or out of order, a range that shares nothing with what the field reads, naming the signal's unit or saying "the signal's" when it has none); a number past the double range and `NaN` refused by the reader; text, hex and enum signals refuse the key, a computed signal takes it with no field to judge it against; the accessor finds a def of the file's own by address, and gives NULL for a def with none, the base set's defs, a copy, another file's def and NULL arguments; the set's bytes are the same with and without a gauge; `x-gauge` stays ignored. A plugin variant takes the gauge of the signal's last file (a later file with none leaves none), and the variant's text, what a recording copies as `signals.json`, loads back with it. `havne-cli` writes a `gauge` object, only the levels given and only for a def with one, on `signal_def` lines (`signals check`, `plugins check`) and the `signals` line of `stream --values`. Python: `SignalFile.gauge`, `Plugin.gauge` (a folder plugin, each variant its own), `havne.Gauge`, None for no such signal or none given. The schema and the loader give one verdict on each edge case and take the same keys. Mutation-checked 2026-11-09 under GCC (34: `min` not below `max` taken either way, a level past either end, levels equal or out of order, a range under or over the field taken, one meeting either end refused, no flag set or the wrong one, the unit unnamed, the no-unit words empty, an unknown key or a gauge that isn't an object taken, a computed or number signal's gauge unread, a copy or any def matched, an enumeration taking it; `havne-cli`'s gauge left off the def line, either level always written, the wrong level's value, each of the three callers passing none; Python's levels unflagged, `Plugin.gauge` ignoring the variant; the schema's `max` optional, a level misnamed) |

| On arbitrary defs and payloads, the decoder returns only its defined codes, gives the same answer twice (NaN bits included), writes nothing unless it succeeds, and reads nothing past `payload_len`; a def the validator accepts fails only on the payload's bounds or its mux key. On arbitrary sets, the set validator returns only defined codes, and its `ecuio_validation_t` matches the return and names the element its code names. On arbitrary `?did=` text, the `uds-generic` builder answers OK or `BAD_URI` with a terminated detail, and a success is a valid set within every limit whose polled head derives one request per def and whose scanned DIDs all fit the engine's scan. |---|---|
| A recording in core ([ADR-0049](adr/0049-recordings-in-core.md)): open makes the folder (named for its second, a label's characters reduced, `-n` for a second recorder in the same second) and its files, and refuses what it must; nothing is written until a session attaches; every frame and value a session dispatches goes in, values with a `.` under any locale, an enum's raw number in `signals.csv` and its labels in the header (`events.log`'s format 3), once for each name and after its first signal line, so a number and an enumeration of one name both open, a line break in a name, unit, text value, marker or label written as a space, and a replay's recording holds every frame of its source with its `frame flags` line; a full ring drops records and counts them, with a `drop` line at the next record's time; a link transition is a `link` line at its time, carried past a full ring and written at close if still carried; reply timing goes to `latency.csv` every 10 s, the last snapshot waiting a second for room; notes outlive the session and stay one line; a failed write stops the recording, never the session; a sleep of 1 s or more is an `asleep` line; the writer wakes ten times a second when idle and syncs every 2 s; nothing allocated per frame while recording; an open refuses each bad argument, and one that runs out of memory at any step is answered `ECUIO_ERR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` with the recorder freed and nothing thrown | `RecorderTest.*`, `CoreTest.WorkersAllocateNothingPerFrame`, `Elm327SessionTest.ARecordingHoldsTheLinksTransitionsAndReplyTiming`. CI's `linux-appimage` builds it on Ubuntu 24.04 and smokes it; that build ran 10 s in WSL's Ubuntu 26.04, Arch and Kali once Mesa and the font libraries were installed on the last two (2026-10-29). With a window under WSLg (Windows 11's Wayland and X server, through XWayland), CI's build of `bd1184e` on all three, each with fresh settings (2026-10-30): Developer mode and recording on, `mock://demo` connected, the Dashboard, Live and Activity pages as expected, a resize, and closing the window ended the process. On a Linux machine's own desktop, v0.3.4's release AppImage on Xubuntu 26.10 (Xfce, X11; 2026-10-30): it started without a FUSE workaround, `mock://demo` ran with recording on, and the recording landed in `~/Documents/Havne/recordings/`. The aarch64 AppImage (ADR-0035's appendix of 2026-11-05): CI's `linux-appimage (aarch64)` builds it on the ARM runner (`ubuntu-24.04-arm`, Qt's `linux_gcc_arm64` kit, linuxdeploy's aarch64 builds) and smokes it; that build (run 42111727555, 2026-11-05) ran 10 s offscreen on a Raspberry Pi 3 (Kali Linux, glibc 2.42), and so did the run before's. The pinned runtime (2026-11-07, WSL's Ubuntu 26.04, x86_64): the pack's runtime reports `dd6cebe` and the AppImage ran 10 s; without `LDAI_RUNTIME_FILE` the pack fails, its runtime `continuous`'s `fedfad5`, as v0.6.0's AppImage reports; a notice without the runtime's line, a `runtime-sources.sh` naming another release and a wrong runtime SHA-256 each fail it; the component list's SBOM validates with `pyspdxtools`. The aarch64 pin is GitHub's digest, unrun until a CI run. |
| The core packages (ADR-0035, `packaging/linux/make-core-packages.sh`): a deb, its `-dbgsym`, a tarball and its `-debug` tarball; the stripped `havne-cli` keeps its build ID, the three USDT probes and a debug link, with no debug info left; no internal header and no build path ships; the deb's `Depends` from `dpkg-shlibdeps` | Built in WSL's Ubuntu 26.04 (2026-10-29). CI's `linux-packages` builds them on Ubuntu 24.04, and `tests/package_check.sh` installs both debs, runs `havne-cli` from `PATH`, builds `tests/package/consumer.c` (a C program running a mock session) through `find_package`, `pkg-config` and the unpacked tarball, and checks `gdb` finds the debug info by build ID. The arm64 ones (`havne-core_X.Y.Z_arm64.deb` and the rest): CI's `linux-packages (aarch64)` builds and checks them the same way on the ARM runner, and the release skill's `verify_linux.sh` on a Raspberry Pi 3 (Kali Linux) passed on that build's files (run 42111727555, 2026-11-05): the debs' package, version and architecture, the files each holds, the deb's `havne-cli` streaming the mock, its debug info by build ID in both debug files. |
| The core packages are reproducible: the same commit builds the same bytes in another folder, locale, time zone, umask, CPU count and clock, and on another machine | `reprotest` with its default variations in WSL's Ubuntu 26.04 (`user_group` skipped, `domain_host` without `sudo`), and a second build in another folder with another locale, time zone and umask: every package identical (2026-10-29). CI's `linux-packages` runs reprotest on every change: two runs of one commit on two runners each reproduced, and the two runners' packages had the same SHA-256s (2026-10-30). On the ARM runner too: both builds of the arm64 packages identical (run 42111727555, 2026-11-05) |
| The AppImage's reproducibility (ADR-0035 section 4: measured, not required): built with `QT_RCC_SOURCE_DATE_OVERRIDE` and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` at the commit's time, two builds of one commit are the same bytes | Two CI runs of `1b457a4` on different runners (2026-10-30): the same SHA-256 and size. With neither, `usr/bin/havne` differs in 46 bytes of `.rodata` (and so its build ID), the modification times Qt's `rcc` records for each embedded file, which are each runner's checkout time (CI, 2026-10-30). With `QT_RCC_SOURCE_DATE_OVERRIDE` alone, `havne` matches but the squashfs's times differ, about 32 KB (WSL, two builds of one folder, 2026-10-29); `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` alone wasn't tried. Not gated: CI builds it once per run, and its bundled libraries follow Ubuntu's updates |
| The release's SBOM (ADR-0035 section 5, `packaging/sbom.py`): one SPDX 2.3 document, each download by name and SHA-256, its components and dependencies; the build jobs' component lists (every DLL in the Windows folder by its version resource, the AppImage's libraries by origin and Ubuntu package, the APK's native libraries and Gradle's resolved tree, the core packages' `Depends`); a library of no known origin fails | `SbomTest.SelfTest` (the merge, the Gradle parse, the APK's classification, the refusals). `pyspdxtools` 0.8.5 validated the self-test's document and one built from the WSL packages (2026-10-29). Each list's code was run against a local build: the Windows folder (Qt 6.12.0 and the Microsoft DLLs; `opengl32sw.dll` has no version), the AppImage's AppDir in WSL (Qt, ICU 73, 31 Ubuntu packages), the local APK's Gradle tree (24 Java libraries). The release's `publish` job builds and validates the whole document; its first run is the next release's dry run |
| The downloads' notices (LGPL-3.0 section 4: Qt's notice and both texts with each copy): the AppImage's `usr/share/doc/havne/` (Havne's license, `packaging/notices/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, the LGPL-3.0, GPL-3.0, Qt-third-party and ICU texts, and the common licenses the bundled Ubuntu packages' copyright files name) and linuxdeploy's `usr/share/doc/<package>/copyright` for each bundled package, checked by `make-appimage.sh` before it packs, with the notice naming the Qt and ICU packed; the APK's `assets/havne/` (the license, the notice, the LGPL-3.0, GPL-3.0, Apache-2.0 and Qt-third-party texts), committed copies `NoticesTest.TheApkCarriesEachNoticeAsItsSource` holds to their sources byte for byte; every notice holding `core/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`'s sections (`NoticesTest.EachNoticeCarriesCoresData`); each license file the notices cite in the tree (`NoticesTest.EachCitedLicenseIsInTheTree`) and each bundled font named (`NoticesTest.EachBundledFontIsNamed`) | `tests/notices_match.cmake` under ctest, mutation-checked 2026-11-07 (5: a copy changed, missing or with no source; core's text missing from either notice). The AppImage built in WSL's Ubuntu 26.04 (2026-11-07): the 32 bundled packages' copyright files and 12 common licenses, the files in the packed AppImage, two packs the same bytes, the AppDir otherwise the same as one linuxdeploy run's; its notices section mutation-checked over that AppDir (6: a bundled package's copyright file gone, the license, the common licenses or the ICU text left out; another Qt or ICU named; each fails the pack). The local Debug APK (2026-11-07) lists the five files under `assets/havne/` |
| The v0.3.1 release zip, as a user gets it: its SHA-256 matches `SHA256SUMS.txt`, `gh attestation verify` passes, and after SmartScreen's "Run anyway" the extracted `havne.exe` runs `mock://demo`; with no VX attached, connecting `j2534://OBDX Pro - VX` fails with "the adapter's driver refused: PassThruOpen returned status 34 (vendor-specific)" (v0.3.1's wording); with the VX on USB and no car, the session opens through the x86 surrogate (battery 0.0 V, the VX's firmware and DLL versions), the supported-PID query goes unanswered and the link goes to requests failing; disconnects are clean | BENCH, 2026-10-25, the `-rc1` dry run and the `v0.3.1` download (the activity log read back in the session, not committed), on the dev machine, which has Visual Studio. The `v0.3.1` zip also ran on a PC without Visual Studio: the simulated vehicle drove, the Activity page showed frames and Live filled with mock values; no adapter was connected there (Gaps) |

## Suites in detail

| Binary | Suites | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| `test_core` | `CoreTest`, `VinTest`, `JsonTest`, `RecorderTest`, `RecordingRowsTest`, `RecordingTest`, `ConditionSessionTest`, `ConditionRecordingTest`, `ArmedRecorderTest`, `CheckRulesTest`, `RecordingCheckTest`, `TempNameTest`, `ObdbImportTest`, `Sha256Test`, `ScrubFramesTest`, `CsvExportTest`, `FolderExportTest`, `HealthTest`, `CsvImportTest`, `CsvGuessTest`, `QueueTest`, `RecordingFramesTest`, `FramesReadTest`, `DefIndexTest`, `CandumpTest`, `ImportTest`, `ScrubTest`, `ExportTest` | Nothing (Qt-free). Scratch files and folders are named for the process and the test (`tests/temp_name.h`, which `test_elm327` and `test_obd2` use too), so runs from two checkouts at once never share one. `QueueTest` (`tests/test_queue.cpp`) feeds sessions that never start through dispatch, and runs a `mock://` worker with readers on threads of their own for the close cases. `RecorderTest` records sessions it feeds by hand into folders of its own in the temporary directory; `RecordingTest` writes recordings for the reader there, an hour of the replay fixture's values among them (`tests/recording_hour.h`); `CheckRulesTest` feeds the frames check row by row, and `RecordingCheckTest` checks folders, the replay fixture with one of each finding put in among them |
| `test_signals` | `SignalDecodeTest`, `SignalsTest`, `SignalTablesTest`, `ComputedTest`, `SignalFileTest`, `PluginTest`, `PluginWrapTest`, `ConditionFileTest`, `ConditionEvalTest` | Nothing (Qt-free) |
| `test_c_abi` (ctest name `c_abi`) | Every public header compiles as ISO C11 with extensions off (`-pedantic` on GCC and Clang) and links unmangled; layout asserts | A C compiler |
| `test_c_abi_shared` (ctest name `c_abi_shared`) | `tests/test_c_abi.c` again, linked against the shared core: each call resolves through its export table (internal hooks left out) | The shared core (`HAVNE_SHARED_CORE`, off on Android and iOS) |
| `test_bridge` | `BridgeTest`, `RecordingViewTest`, `ReadOnceTest`, `FrameFilterTest`, `ObdbImportTest`, `RecordingExportTest`, `ViewerFramesTest` | Qt Core and Qt Network. Compiles its `app/` sources directly (`sessionbridge.cpp`, `recordingview.cpp`, `recordingimport.cpp`, `healthreport.cpp`, `framefilter.cpp`, the stores and the rest `tests/CMakeLists.txt` lists), so they must stay free of Qt Gui and Quick. Its `main` puts `HAVNE_SETTINGS_DIR` in a scratch folder, so no case's signal or plugin store is the user's. `RecordingViewTest` opens `tests/fixtures/recording/` and scratch folders |
| `test_qml` | One ctest case per `tests/qml/tst_*.qml` file, `QmlTest.<name>` (`QmlTest.theme` for `tst_theme.qml`); a theme split over several files (`tst_dashboard{,_pages,_panel}.qml`, `tst_driveview{,_sheet,_platform}.qml` and the like) is one entry for each file, so a citation of `QmlTest.dashboard{,_pages,_panel}` or `QmlTest.driveview{,_sheet,_platform}` in this document names the family's entries. `tst_dashedit.qml` and the `tst_dashedit_*.qml` files are one entry each (`QmlTest.dashedit`, `QmlTest.dashedit_panel`, ...). `tst_dashedit*.qml`, `tst_settings*.qml`, `tst_phone_*.qml`, `tst_recordings*.qml`, `tst_dashboard*.qml`, `tst_dashboardbook*.qml`, `tst_driveview*.qml`, `tst_chartlayout*.qml` and `tst_liveview*.qml` derive from a kit file (`DashEditKit.qml`, `SettingsKit.qml`, `PhoneKit.qml`, `RecordingsKit.qml`, `DashboardKit.qml`, `DashboardBookKit.qml`, `DriveViewKit.qml`, `ChartLayoutKit.qml`, `LiveViewKit.qml`) that holds their shared helpers (`tst_phone_<part>.qml` is the entry `QmlTest.phone_<part>`); a kit's name never starts with `tst_`, so it is not an entry. | Qt Quick. Links the app's QML module (`app_ui`). The quiet, fault and requests cases need the fake DLL (Windows) and skip elsewhere. |
| `test_j2534_discovery` | `J2534DiscoveryTest` | Windows |
| `test_j2534_load` | `FakeFixture` | Windows and the fake DLL |
| `test_j2534_stream` | `J2534StreamTest`, `J2534QueryParamTest`, `J2534AdoptionTest` | Windows and the fake DLL |
| `test_j2534_health` | `J2534HealthTest` | Windows, the fake DLL and the x64 surrogate beside it |
| `test_j2534_connect` | `J2534ConnectTest`, `J2534SurrogateConnectTest` | Windows, the fake DLL and the x64 surrogate beside it |
| `test_j2534_surrogate` | `J2534SurrogateTest` | Windows, the fake DLL and the surrogate exe |
| `test_j2534_routing` | `J2534RoutingTest` | Windows. The cross-arch case skips unless `ECUIO_FAKE_DLL` is set. |
| `test_obd2` | `Obd2SetTest`, `Obd2DecodeTest`, `Obd2MuxTest`, `Obd2ReplyTest`, `RequestEngineTest`, `UdsGenericTest`, `Obd2TableTest`, `FakeEcuTest`, `DiagnosticsTest`, `ClearTest`, `DtcTitleTest`, `InspectionTest`, `SimEcuTest`, `SimDidsTest`, `SimAskTest`, `SimRecordingTest`, `PidSupportTest`, `RequestTimingTest`, `SessionControlTest` | Nothing (Qt-free). `Obd2TableTest` reads the Service 01 table, `tests/fixtures/obd2/service01.csv` |
| `test_elm327` | `Elm327ProtocolTest`, `Elm327SessionTest`, `Elm327LogTest`, `SerialPortsTest`, `Elm327TcpTest`, `Elm327StreamTest`, and on Linux and macOS `Elm327PortTest` | Nothing (Qt-free). The session cases put the fake ELM327 (`tests/fake_elm327.h`) behind the transport's port opener; the port cases put it on a pseudo-terminal, the TCP cases behind a listener on 127.0.0.1, the stream cases behind a registered app-fed stream with a thread playing the app |
| `test_vcan` | `VcanTest`, `SocketCanBusTest`, `SocketCanRequestTest`, `SocketCanFaultTest`, `IsoTpConformanceTest`, `SimBusTest` | Linux (Qt-free), and `vcan0` up with `can-isotp` loaded; each case skips without them, and fails under `ECUIO_VCAN_REQUIRED=1` (CI's `linux-build`). `VcanTest`: the simulated bus itself (`tests/vcan_bus.h`), the fakes' ECU answering through the kernel's ISO-TP, and a recording played onto the bus. `SocketCanBusTest`: the `socketcan://` transport on that bus (ADR-0032); its interface-down case needs root and runs again under `sudo` on CI (`ECUIO_VCAN_ROOT_REQUIRED=1`). `SocketCanRequestTest`: `socketcan://` requesting through core's ISO-TP against that ECU (ADR-0040). `SocketCanFaultTest`: the same against the test car's recording as the ECU (`tests/vcan_recording_ecu.h`), its replies broken at the ISO-TP level (ADR-0041 section 4). `IsoTpConformanceTest`: core's ISO-TP against the kernel's |
| `test_socketcan` | `SocketCanTest`, `IsoTpTest`, `SimTest` | Nothing (Qt-free), every platform. SocketCAN's rules with no bus (`core/src/socketcan_wire.h`, with the kernel's values and the link's judges in `socketcan_link.h`): interface names, `?filter=`, canned netlink messages and the listen-only judgment, kernel frames, the clock, and the URI's refusals; on Linux also the loopback and a missing interface. ISO-TP's receiver and sender (`core/src/isotp.h`, ADR-0040) |
| (ctest only) | `GoldenTest.SelfTest`, `AbiReportTest.SelfTest`, `ApiCheckTest.SelfTest`, `VpicWmiTest.SelfTest`, `DtcTitlesTest.SelfTest`, `RecordingCompareTest.SelfTest`, `SchemaKeysTest.EachObjectsKeysAreItsLoaders`, `ExportsTest.SelfTest`, `PlainTextPinTest.SelfTest`, `PlainTextPinTest.EveryTextSetsItsFormat` | Python 3 (left out without it). The golden-output comparator (`tests/golden.py`), the ABI check's judge of an abidiff report (`scripts/abi_report.py`) and its comparison of the headers (`scripts/api_check.py`), the VIN table's generator (`scripts/vpic_wmi.py`) and the trouble-code titles' (`scripts/dtc_titles.py`) and the recording folders' comparator (`tests/recording_compare.py`), and the shared core's export and release rules (`scripts/exports_check.py`), each over its own cases; `tests/schema_keys.py` compares each `schema/` object's keys with the `only_keys` call that checks it, and the loaders' word lists with the schemas' enums; `tests/plaintext_pin.py` scans `app/qml` for each Text and Label's `textFormat` (only `Text.PlainText`, or the one value its allow list names for a file and objectName), for a stock control or `ToolTip.text` that could draw outside text as markup, and for a rich `TextEdit` or `TextArea`, with a self-test over made-up files |
| `test_cli` | `CliLineTest`, `CliRingTest`, `CliSleepTest`, `CliDiagnosticsTest`, `CliVinTest`, `CliDtcTest`, `CliInspectionTest`, `CliSimTest`, `CliMarkLinesTest`, `CliViewTest`, `CliInfoTest`, `CliSignalFileTest`, `CliPluginTest`, `CliHealthTest`, `CliScanTest`, `CliClearTest`, `CliCanTest`, `CliTimingTest`, `CliRefusedTest`, `CliRecordingSignalsTest`, `CliViewFramesTest`, `CliElm327Test`, `CliTransferTest`, `CliPartialTest`, `CliFramesTest`, `CliSessionTest` | Nothing (Qt-free). `havne-cli`'s line formatting its worker-to-output ring, its sleep rule, its `diagnostics` line and its `vin`, `identity`, `dtc` and `inspection` lines, the lines typed into `stream --record`, `view`'s grammar, axis and lines, `info`'s lines, `frames`' arguments and lines, a signal file's lines and a URI's profile, and a plugin's lines and the rule a fixture passes by (`cli/`, header-only; it links core for the last two) |
| (ctest only) | `CliGoldenTest`, `CliUsageTest`, `CliSignalTest`, `CliReplayTest`, `CliRecordTest`, `CliViewRunTest`, `CliObdbImportTest`, `CliPluginRunTest`, `CliElm327RunTest`, `CliClearRunTest`, `CliTransferRunTest`, `CliHealthRunTest`, `CliExitTest`, `CliConnectTest`, `CliPathTest`, `CliRefusedRunTest`, `SchemaFixturesTest` | `havne-cli` run as a child process. `CliTransferRunTest` (`tests/cli_transfer*.py`, Python 3) imports a candump log, reads it through obd2, exports it as pcapng and reads it back with Python's `struct`; its tshark case (skipped without tshark) has tshark's ISO-TP dissector reassemble the made-up frames, and its candump case (Linux) logs the simulated car on `vcan0` with `candump -L` and compares the import with a direct session. `CliElm327RunTest` (`tests/cli_elm327.py`, Python 3) talks ELM327 to `sim --elm327` as a host does, reads it through core's own `elm327://` session, and through python-OBD when installed (skip 77 otherwise). `CliClearRunTest` (`tests/cli_clear.py`, Python 3) clears the fake DLL's ECU on Windows (running, standing, refused, moving, the word on moving) and the simulated ECU on `vcan0` on Linux (skip 77 without it). `SchemaFixturesTest` (`tests/schema_fixtures.py`, the `jsonschema` package from `.github/jsonschema-requirements.txt`; skipped without it unless `ECUIO_SCHEMA_REQUIRED=1`, as CI's `core-and-tests` sets) validates every committed signal file, manifest, data file and fixture file against `schema/`, and gets one verdict from the schema and `havne-cli`'s checks for each refused file and edge case. `CliPluginRunTest` (`tests/cli_plugin_check.py`, Python 3, Linux) runs `plugins check --on vcan0` on every plugin in `plugins/` (skipped without vcan0 unless `ECUIO_VCAN_REQUIRED`, serial as `test_vcan` is). `CliObdbImportTest` (`tests/cli_obdb_import.py`, Python 3) imports the synthetic set in `tests/fixtures/obdb/` and compares the lines with a golden and the file written with `tests/golden/obdb/`, and an `--out` that can't be written. `CliRecordTest` (`tests/cli_record.py`, Python 3) records through `stream --record` and compares the folder with `tests/golden/recording/` (`tests/recording_compare.py`); `CliViewRunTest` (`tests/cli_view.py`, Python 3) views `tests/fixtures/recording/`. The golden runs need Python 3 and compare its whole output with `tests/golden/cli/`; the usage cases run under `cmake -P` (`tests/cli_exit.cmake`); the signal case is SIGINT and SIGTERM on Linux and macOS (`tests/cli_signal.sh`), Ctrl+Break on Windows (`tests/cli_ctrl_break.py`, Python 3; skipped without a console) |
| (ctest only) | `PythonTest.<file>`, one per `python/tests/test_*.py` | The `havne` package under pytest (`python/tests/run.py`: exit 77, a skip, without pytest or when every case in a file skipped; a failure instead under `ECUIO_PYTEST_REQUIRED=1`, as CI's `core-and-tests`, `win-build`, `macos-build` and `linux-build` set), against the tree's own shared core (`HAVNE_LIBRARY`). Registered where the tests' Python has the tree's pointer size: the x64 trees, and `build-win32` when configured with a 32-bit Python, which `scripts/verify.sh`'s cross-arch step runs against the x86 core, fake and surrogate. `test_did_scan` (the mock everywhere; on `vcan0` the simulated ECU's second module against the unbound sim's own answers) and `test_isotp_fuzz` (Linux, `vcan0`: 200 cases, one replayed alone, no ECU, the ECU closed mid-run, a second ECU already on the bus, `lo` refused) take `RESOURCE_LOCK vcan0` with `test_vcan` |
| (ctest only) | `WheelTest.SelfTest`, `WheelTest.Installed` | `packaging/python/make_wheel.py selftest` (made-up PE, ELF and Mach-O files and a made-up package: the tags, each refusal, `RECORD`, two builds byte for byte). `tests/wheel_installed.py` (64-bit trees with the shared core, not the sanitizer trees): the tree's own library packed twice to the same bytes, then `packaging/python/check_wheel.py`, release.yml's own check: a fresh venv, `pip install --no-index`, `HAVNE_LIBRARY` and the surrogates' variables unset, the library in `site-packages` (Linux's single `libecuio.so.<soname>`), `read_pids.py mock://`, and on Windows the fake DLL through the wheel's x64 surrogate and the Win32 tree's fake through its x86 one where `build-win32` exists (not in CI); exit 77 without venv or pip, a failure under `ECUIO_PYTEST_REQUIRED=1` |
| (ctest only) | `DiscoveryTimeoutTest.EveryDiscoveryWaits`, `RequiredTest.ThePythonTestsAreRegistered` | CMake alone. `tests/discovery_timeout.cmake`: every `gtest_discover_tests` call in `tests/CMakeLists.txt` names `DISCOVERY_TIMEOUT 60`, since discovery runs each binary at build time and a cold runner can take more than the default 5 s. `tests/python_registered.cmake`: configure registered at least one `PythonTest` case, and `WheelTest.Installed` where a 64-bit tree without sanitizers should, so a probe that failed can't leave them out unseen: a failure under `ECUIO_PYTEST_REQUIRED=1` (CI's test jobs), a skip otherwise |
| (ctest only) | `ExampleTest.isotp_fuzz_cli`, `ExampleTest.notebook` | `tests/example_isotp_fuzz.py <havne-cli>` (Linux, `vcan0`, `RESOURCE_LOCK vcan0`): `python/examples/isotp_fuzz.py --ecu-cli`, so `havne-cli sim` is the ECU and must end with exit 0 and its end line not failed; outside pytest, so `core-sanitizers` runs it on its ASan `havne-cli`; skips without `vcan0` unless `ECUIO_VCAN_REQUIRED=1`; its own bound (240 s) under ctest's, dumping the fuzzer's stack on a hang. `tests/example_notebook.py`: `python/examples/ecu_states.ipynb` run top to bottom through `nbconvert` in a kernelspec of the test's own (the test's Python), its last cell's check included; the run's modules imported first in a child Python under a bound of their own (600 s), so a cold read from a busy disk never reads as a hung notebook, then the notebook under 240 s; exit 77 without scikit-learn, numpy, nbconvert, nbclient or ipykernel, which no CI job installs, so it runs locally (`build-py-venv`) |

## The BENCH and Vehicle sessions

**BENCH.** First formal session complete, 2026-10-16, quiet bus ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-16.md)); informal quiet-bus follow-up 2026-10-20 ([note](bench-sessions/2026-10-20.md)); quiet-bus run of the vehicle session's URIs with recording, 2026-10-22 ([sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-22.md)), and again on the laptop that goes to the car, 2026-10-25 ([sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-25-bench.md)); the VX as an ELM327 over USB, no bus, 2026-10-27: refused at `AT Z` ([sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-27-elm327-bench.md)), then every phase passed, a cable pull included ([sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-27-elm327-bench-2.md)); the later sessions, the SocketCAN desk bus among them, are listed in [bench-prep.md](bench-sessions/bench-prep.md)

**Vehicle.** Sessions on one car, its engine ECU on `0x7E8`: 2026-10-22, ignition off, engine off and idling ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-22-vehicle.md)); 2026-10-25, the same plus the request engine, the probe, identification, `uds-generic` and an ignition cycle ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-25-vehicle.md)); 2026-10-27, through the ELM327 transport: the OBDLink MX+ over Bluetooth through a cold start and idle, the values as in the J2534 sessions, and the VX in ELM mode through a start and idle ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-10-27-elm327-vehicle.md)); 2026-10-31, the full `obd2` set and the trouble codes through both adapters ([VX](bench-sessions/2026-10-31-vehicle.md), [MX+](bench-sessions/2026-10-31-elm327-vehicle.md)); 2026-11-01, several PIDs per request, more than one ECU, DID scans, the timer A/B and the first clears, through the VX ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-01-vehicle.md)) and the MX+ relayed by the test phone ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-01-android.md)); 2026-11-02, the VX re-runs of that day's fixes ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-02-vehicle.md)); 2026-11-03, a real CAN controller under SocketCAN listening beside the VX ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-03-socketcan-car.md)); 2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05, both ECUs in one session through both adapters, and listening beside the VX ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-04-two-ecu-car.md), [re-run](bench-sessions/2026-11-05-two-ecu-car-rerun.md)); 2026-11-06, a signal file's DIDs on two modules ([session sheet](bench-sessions/2026-11-06-own-signals-car.md)). Run sheets [vehicle-runsheet.md](bench-sessions/vehicle-runsheet.md) and [elm327-vehicle-runsheet.md](bench-sessions/elm327-vehicle-runsheet.md)

## Gaps in detail

- **A plugin has not met a car, and the analysis log has run on a car
  over ELM327 only.** Plugins' data tier is checked against the
  simulated car; `havne-cli log` and the app's Start log have read the
  test car through the MX+ and the VX over ELM327, and not through J2534
  or SocketCAN ([ADR-0056](adr/0056-analysis-log.md)'s appendix "the log
  on a car, over ELM327").

- **What the scrub can't see, and what it can refuse.** A scrubbed
  export, the Activity page's Export CSV and the health report find the
  car's identity by the values its identification replies and text
  signals held, and by a VIN's shape. A VIN run together with other
  letters or digits is found only when its check digit holds, and a VIN
  sent raw, in frames that aren't ISO-TP (a counter byte first, say),
  isn't found unless one frame holds it whole. The scrub can also
  refuse a folder copy whose marker label is 17 or more characters of
  the VIN alphabet. ADR-0054's appendix "the C API as built" lists
  these limits.

- **The security store's flush and its writers.** No test shows the
  flush reached the disk (a power cut is BENCH-only), no test has two
  processes write one store at once (the merge is shown with a file
  changed between a load and a save), and `open_existing` reads an
  unreachable share by Windows' code with no share in a test. A session's
  use of the store (which thread, the mark before the seed on the wire)
  is not built yet.

- **The notebook doesn't run in CI.** `ExampleTest.notebook` skips in
  every CI job, which carries no scientific stack; it runs locally in
  `build-py-venv`.

- **The app has no Import button.** A CSV or candump import made by
  `havne-cli` or Python is listed once its folder is moved into the
  recordings folder (v0.7.0).

- **The Python declarations' signedness isn't checked.** The generated
  ctypes types are held to the headers' sizes and offsets and to the
  generator's own output, not to each C type's signedness: a `uint32_t`
  mapped to `c_int32` would keep every size and pass.

- **ThreadSanitizer runs only by hand.** CI's sanitizer job runs ASan
  and UBSan. The pull queue's close races are ThreadSanitizer's to
  catch (ASan doesn't see accesses inside glibc's pthread calls); it
  ran in WSL over 30 repeats when the queue was built, and no CI job
  repeats it.

- **A sleep in session time has run on Android only.** No CI runner or
  WSL VM can sleep. The test phone's sleeps counted
  ([2026-10-29-android-2](bench-sessions/2026-10-29-android-2.md));
  a Linux machine's suspend hasn't run, and Windows and Apple rest on
  their documentation (BENCH prep #27).

- **The Win32 serial port has no automated test.** Windows has no
  built-in virtual COM pair, so `elm327_port.cpp`'s Win32 half (the
  `\\.\COMn` open, the DCB, the read timeouts) is verified at BENCH
  and on the vehicle only: the VX's bench session ran through it, a
  cable pull and the reopen included
  (docs/bench-sessions/2026-10-27-elm327-bench-2.md), and the vehicle
  session ran it over a Bluetooth COM port (the MX+) and the VX's USB
  port (docs/bench-sessions/2026-10-27-elm327-vehicle.md). The POSIX half runs
  over a pseudo-terminal in CI ([ADR-0026](adr/0026-elm327-transport.md)
  section 8).
- **The surrogate pipe's protections are tested on the pipe, not
  against another user.** `J2534PipeTest` checks the DACL, the one
  instance, the first-instance flag and remote clients on the pipe each
  session makes, but nothing connects as another user, and nothing looks
  for a handle leaked to the child past the one-handle inheritance list.
- **Discovery's UTF-16 registry read is untested.** The non-ASCII
  tests start from a path; reading one from `FUNCTIONLIBRARY` would
  need an HKLM entry, which needs elevation.
- **The release zip has run on a PC without Visual Studio only on
  mock data.** There, v0.3.1's zip ran `mock://demo`, and frames and
  Live's values arrived. No adapter has been connected on such a PC,
  so neither surrogate has run on one. v0.3.2's zip, which adds the
  x64 surrogate, hasn't run there at all.
  Each surrogate links the C runtime statically, and `stage.ps1`
  checks that every DLL `havne.exe` imports ships in the zip or is
  part of Windows, but both checks run on the dev machine.
- **GCC and Clang warnings fail only CI's compilers.** CI sets
  `HAVNE_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS`, so a warning in first-party code fails
  the GCC or Clang job that sees it, but each compiler warns about
  different things: a runner image's newer compiler can fail a commit
  that passed before, and the linux skill's WSL tree (a newer GCC and
  Clang than CI's) warns without failing unless the variable is set.
  MSVC's warnings never fail a build; `scripts/verify.sh` counts them
  (0).

- **The x64 surrogate has no real-hardware coverage.** The one J2534
  adapter on hand, the OBDX Pro VX, ships an x86 DLL, so real vendor
  code has run only through the x86 surrogate. The x64 one, which every
  x64 vendor DLL would load in, has met only the x64 fake (in every
  CI run).
- **Real-hardware coverage is two adapters and one vehicle.** The
  quiet-bus BENCH sessions (2026-10-16, 2026-10-20, 2026-10-22,
  2026-10-25) and the J2534 vehicle sessions on one car ran the OBDX
  Pro VX through the surrogate; the ELM327 sessions ran the OBDLink MX+
  and the VX in ELM mode. The car's engine ECU answers on 11-bit
  `0x7E8`, and a second ECU on `0x7E9`
  ([2026-11-01-vehicle](bench-sessions/2026-11-01-vehicle.md)). Still
  unverified against real hardware: 29-bit addressing, a second car,
  and any other vendor's J2534 statuses (the VX never returned
  `ERR_TIMEOUT`).
- **The request engine has met two ECUs, together in one session.** On
  the car (2026-10-25) it paced Service 01 at about 26 requests a second
  with every reply but one in 7,196 in before the next request, and
  Service 0x22 at about 13. Both of the car's ECUs in one session, a
  DID each, ran through the VX (each ECU's requests waiting only for
  their own replies, about 32 a second each) and through the MX+ (the
  two taking turns, about 19 a second each)
  ([2026-11-04-two-ecu-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-04-two-ecu-car.md)).
  A "response pending" reply and a late negative reply are tested
  against the fake only: the car sent no `78` and refused only in step.
- **The probe has met one car.** It walked six bitmaps there and
  dropped only `01 5C`, as the first session's bitmaps predicted, and
  a cold connect dropped nothing. A car that refuses the bitmap
  request itself, or answers it with a negative reply, hasn't been
  seen.
- **`uds-generic` has met one car's default sessions.** The scan of
  `F180-F1FF`, three text DIDs, two 1,024-DID blocks through the VX
  ([2026-11-02-vehicle](bench-sessions/2026-11-02-vehicle.md)) and a
  sweep of the engine ECU's 64,509 remaining DIDs through the MX+
  ([2026-11-01-android](bench-sessions/2026-11-01-android.md)) ran on
  the car; every refusal was `7F 22 31`. The second ECU refused F190
  the same way, and its scan of `F180-F1FF` and `D800-DFFF` through the
  VX found 16 DIDs, its values named by their `0x7E9` prefix
  ([2026-11-04-two-ecu-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-04-two-ecu-car.md)).
  Other refusal codes (`11`, `33`) are tested against the fake only.
- **The extended session has met one module.** The test car's engine
  ECU answered `10 03` and read in the session through the VX and the
  MX+ ([2026-11-11-ext-session-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-11-ext-session-car.md)).
  Tester present held it through quiet gaps
  ([2026-11-11-quiet-session-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-11-quiet-session-car.md)),
  but no module's session has been read back and no DID that exists
  only in the extended session has been read. The closes, through both
  adapters, left one `10 01` and one `50 01` each
  ([2026-11-18-session-close-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-18-session-close-car.md);
  BENCH prep #36 in
  [bench-prep.md](bench-sessions/bench-prep.md)).
- **Listening to another tester has met two adapters.** The MX+'s
  `AT MA` beside the VX on a Y-splitter passed the engine ECU's replies,
  each matching the VX's in order and bytes, but printed a multi-frame
  reply's First Frame and none of its Consecutive Frames, under the
  setup's `AT CFC1`
  ([2026-11-04-two-ecu-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-04-two-ecu-car.md))
  and under `AT CFC0` and six more setups
  ([2026-11-05-two-ecu-car-rerun](bench-sessions/2026-11-05-two-ecu-car-rerun.md),
  BENCH prep #19, Known Issue #41). The VX in ELM mode, on USB, heard
  the MX+'s session's replies, every one of 1,258 paired in order by its
  bytes, all 1,195 segmented ones with them
  ([2026-11-08-linux-car](bench-sessions/2026-11-08-linux-car.md)). The
  fake ELM327 models the MX+'s monitor (`monitor_eats_cfs`: a First Frame
  prints and its Consecutive Frames don't, under either `AT CFC` setting).
- **Identification has met one ECU.** On the car every item settled
  (four Service 09 items and two DIDs answered, seven DIDs refused),
  and its replies of up to 27 bytes arrived whole. An ECU with several
  calibration IDs, or one that leaves an item unanswered while awake,
  hasn't been seen.
- **Trouble codes have met one ECU, with none stored.** Through both
  adapters the test car's empty lists, readiness and freeze-frame
  bitmap read as SAE J1979 says
  ([2026-10-31-elm327-vehicle](bench-sessions/2026-10-31-elm327-vehicle.md));
  a stored code, a multi-frame list and the freeze frame's values are
  tested against the fakes' ECU and the simulated car only, and a
  second scan tool's reading beside Havne's is BENCH prep #28.
- **Clearing has met one ECU, with no codes stored.** Through the MX+,
  the test car's engine ECU answered `04` with `44` with the engine off
  and running, and the read after showed its readiness monitors
  incomplete
  ([2026-11-01-android](bench-sessions/2026-11-01-android.md)). A
  stored code erased, a permanent one kept, a clear through the VX and
  a second scan tool's reading are BENCH prep #29's rest; the fakes and
  the simulated car answer `01 0C` and `04` as the code reads SAE
  J1979.
- **Link health is covered against the fake.** The tests above drive
  `ECUIO_FAKE_READ_STATUS`, `ECUIO_FAKE_UNTOUCHED_COUNT_STATUS`,
  `ECUIO_FAKE_WRITE_STATUS` and `ECUIO_FAKE_WRITE_ZERO_COUNT` through
  the worker, in the surrogate. Not
  covered:
  - a hard read failure from real hardware, beyond one. Live runs
    drove every state through the surrogate on the VX: requests
    failing at the bench, ok, quiet and recovery on the vehicle, and
    on 2026-10-25, after the laptop resumed from hibernation, a read
    returning vendor status 27 ("adapter fault", the backoff at its
    cap, a clean disconnect). A failure of that kind mid-session,
    with the adapter still enumerated, hasn't been seen;
  - other vendors' read and write statuses. The VX's are known: an
    empty read returns `ERR_BUFFER_EMPTY` and data comes with
    `NOERROR`, never `ERR_TIMEOUT`; with nothing acknowledging, three
    writes are accepted and the fourth fails `ERR_FAILED`, and on a
    vehicle every write succeeded
    ([bench](bench-sessions/2026-10-22.md),
    [vehicle](bench-sessions/2026-10-22-vehicle.md)).
- **The fake's default read model is simpler than J2534's.** By
  default its `ReadMsgs` returns one message per call whatever
  `NumMsgs` and the timeout ask for, and `STATUS_NOERROR` with zero
  messages on an empty queue. The exact-count stream tests are pinned
  to that. Two opt-in modes hold a read until its batch fills or its
  timeout passes: J2534-1's (`ECUIO_FAKE_SPEC_READ_SEMANTICS`) and the
  VX's (`ECUIO_FAKE_VX`), which is the one with `ERR_BUFFER_EMPTY` on
  a worker read. Neither models filter gating. A vendor that leaves
  its outputs untouched on error is modeled
  (`ECUIO_FAKE_UNTOUCHED_COUNT_STATUS`).
- **The fake never segments a reply.** Every reply is one message,
  so a `START_OF_MESSAGE` indication appears only where a test queues
  one by hand (`IndicationsAheadOfAReplyAreSkipped`).
- **Vendor conformance to J2534-1 v04.04 is known for one vendor.**
  The shared types header is verified against public references, and
  the VX's replies decode, so its struct layout matches. It departs
  from the read rule, though: a short batch comes back with
  `STATUS_NOERROR`, not `ERR_TIMEOUT`. Another vendor can depart
  elsewhere, and only its own device can show it
  ([ADR-0010](adr/0010-j2534-v0404-shared-types.md)).
- **Discovery sees a seeded registry only in CI's coverage job.** On a
  machine with no vendors installed the discovery test checks only the
  shape of an empty result; `scripts/verify.sh` sets
  `ECUIO_EXPECT_J2534_DEVICES=1` on Windows so the dev machine's
  installed driver has to be found. `win-coverage` registers the fake
  DLL in both registry views (`REG_EXPAND_SZ`, an environment-variable
  path) beside three entries discovery must skip (an empty
  `FUNCTIONLIBRARY`, a number, a relative path) and demands it, but a
  test failing under the coverage tool shows only in the job's summary
  line, not as a failed job; `win-build` has no
  vendors and never sees a non-empty result.
- **The mobile apps have no automated run on a device or emulator**
  (Known Issue #2). CI compiles both kits. The simulator smoke shows
  the iOS app starts and draws, but nothing taps through its UI, and
  it skips ordinary pushes. No tests run on either mobile platform,
  because cross builds set `BUILD_TESTING=OFF`. The Android app has
  run only on one phone, by hand
  ([android-phone-runsheet.md](bench-sessions/android-phone-runsheet.md));
  `test_qml` checks Settings and Home at a phone's sizes on the
  desktop. Its Bluetooth (ADR-0028) has run on the phone with no
  adapter and with the MX+ on the car; a BLE adapter has met only the
  picker's made-up services. The background sessions (ADR-0029) held
  on the car, the screen off with the app in front included.
- **Android's documents run only on a phone.** The open dialogs'
  picker (`app/documentpicker.cpp`, Android's ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT) and
  a provider's descriptor route (`openPicked` on a `content://` name)
  have no desktop or CI test: on the desktop the six dialogs are Qt's
  FileDialog, and `QmlTest.documentpicker` and each page's case reach
  the routing through `deliver()`, the call the activity result makes.
  The Android run sheet's documents phase
  ([android-phone-runsheet.md](bench-sessions/android-phone-runsheet.md),
  Phase 10) is the check for any change to the picker, `urlwrite.h` or
  the folder export. Run by hand on the test phone (2026-11-13): each
  of the six dialogs with a file named "name (1)", a plain one and a
  cancel; an export to a taken name, which wrote the "(1)" document; a
  folder export; and a save to a cloud provider with the phone
  offline, which read as saved and uploaded once the network was back.
  A provider that fails only after it has the whole document can't
  tell the app, so such a failure is not seen.
- **CI never launches the desktop app itself.** `test_qml` builds the
  app's window and pages from the same QML module, offscreen, on all
  three desktop platforms, but never runs the `havne` executable, and
  nothing looks at a frame. The manual smoke tier covers that, on the
  dev machine.
- **QML tests read properties, not pixels.** They run offscreen on the
  software renderer and check what each item shows: its color, text
  and visibility. The strip chart's layout is arithmetic the tests run
  (`ChartLayout`), but its drawing, the gauges, layout at each
  width, focus rings and the GPU backends are manual smoke only. Off
  Windows there's no fake vendor DLL, so only the disconnected and ok
  link states run there.

- **A request longer than a frame has gaps the cases do not reach.** No
  sanitizer run of the vcan cases happens outside CI. The worker's
  unechoed-frame, halt and reopen ends of a send have no case on a bus
  (the unit pins `socketcan_send_end`). While a long request goes out and
  another channel has a request due, the SocketCAN worker polls with no
  wait until the send ends: correct output, a busy core for up to 750 ms
  of wait frames. SocketCAN times STmin from a Consecutive Frame's write,
  not its echo, so a frame held by arbitration is followed sooner than
  the ECU asked. A talk answered after 5 s or more of `7F xx 78` is
  followed by a session entry, which discards an unlock. J2534's wait for
  a talk's reply must cover the adapter's whole segmented send and the
  reply, and the fake DLL never segments (BENCH prep #37). The fake DLL
  has no security access until the conversation tier's next part.
